It follows that:

\cont(\Omega_{A_{j}/A_{i}}\otimes L^{\circ})\leq\cont\left({L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{j}}\right>}/{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{i}}\right>}\right).

Thus, it suffices to find morphisms
A[\underline{X_{i}}]\to A[\underline{X_{j}}]
such that (
3.2.l
) commutes and

\lim_{i\in I}\lim_{j\geq i}\cont\left({L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{j}}\right>}/{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{i}}\right>}\right)=0.

Again, we handle this case by case.

(
\aleph
)

Case
\mu\in\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}
:

We may replace
I
with any cofinal subsequence.
In subcase (
\aleph
.d), i.e., when
\Gamma
is discrete,
??
shows that we may replace
I
by a maximal element of
I
and the result is trivial.
The same remark shows that in subcase (
\aleph
.i), i.e., when
\Gamma
is not discrete, we may replace
I
by the subsequence
I^{\prime}=\{(\beta,\ell)\in I\mid\ell=1\}
.
Let
i=(\beta,1)\in I^{\prime},j=(\gamma,1)\in I^{\prime}
.
Then
\underline{X_{i}}=[X,Z,Y_{i}],\underline{X_{j}}=[X,Z,Y_{j}]
and the natural mapping
X\mapsto X,Z\mapsto Z,Y_{i}\mapsto\beta^{-1}\gamma Y_{j}
defines a suitable morphism
A[X,Z,Y_{i}]\to A[X,Z,Y_{j}]
.
So

\frac{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{j}}\right>}{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{i}}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X,\mathrm{d}Z,\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X,\mathrm{d}Z,\mathrm{d}Y_{i}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\beta^{-1}\gamma\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>},

which is a module of content
w(\beta^{-1}\gamma)=w(\beta^{-1}\varphi)-w(\gamma\varphi^{-1})\leq\mu-v_{i}(\beta)
, and

\lim_{(\beta,1)\in I}\lim_{(\gamma,1)\geq(\beta,1)}\mu-v_{i}(\beta)=0.

(
\beth
)

Case
\mu\not\in\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(\infty)
and
\Gamma
is not discrete:

Let
i=(\alpha,\beta)\in I,j=(\gamma,\delta)\in I^{\prime}
.
Then
\underline{X_{i}}=[X_{i},Y_{i}],\underline{X_{j}}=[X_{j},Y_{j}]
and the natural mapping
X_{i}\mapsto\alpha^{-1}\gamma X_{j},Y_{i}\mapsto\beta^{-1}\delta Y_{j}
defines a suitable morphism
A[X_{i},Y_{i}]\to A[X_{j},Y_{j}]
.
So

\frac{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{j}}\right>}{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{i}}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{j},\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{i},\mathrm{d}Y_{i}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\alpha^{-1}\gamma\mathrm{d}X_{j}\right>}\oplus\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\beta^{-1}\delta\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>},

which is a module of content

w(\alpha^{-1}\varphi)-w(\gamma^{-1}\varphi)+w(\beta^{-1}\varphi)-w(\delta^{-1}\varphi)\leq 2\mu-v_{i}(\alpha)-v_{i}(\beta),

and

\lim_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I}\lim_{(\gamma,\delta)\geq(\alpha,\beta)}2\mu-v_{i}(\alpha)-v_{i}(\beta)=0.

(
\gimel
)

Case
\mu\not\in\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}(\infty)
and
\Gamma
is discrete:

Let
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
with
j\geq i
.
Then
\underline{X_{i}}=[X_{i},Y_{i}],\underline{X_{j}}=[X_{j},Y_{j}]
. Let
m,n,m^{\prime},n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
be the unique integers such that

\displaystyle(a_{2i},b_{2i})

\displaystyle=m\cdot(a_{2j},b_{2j})+n(a_{2j+1},b_{2j}+1),

\displaystyle(a_{2i+1},b_{2i+1})

\displaystyle=m^{\prime}\cdot(a_{2j},b_{2j})+n^{\prime}(a_{2j+1},b_{2j}+1).

Note that
mn^{\prime}-m^{\prime}n=1
because
\begin{pmatrix}m&m^{\prime}\\
n&n^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z})^{+}
as it maps a basis of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
to a basis of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with the same orientation.
Then we can take the natural morphism
A[X_{i},Y_{i}]\mapsto A[X_{j},Y_{j}]
induced by
X_{i}\mapsto X_{j}^{m}Y_{j}^{n},Y_{i}\mapsto X_{j}^{m^{\prime}}Y_{j}^{n^{\prime}}
.
So

\frac{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{j}}\right>}{L^{\circ}\cdot\left<\mathrm{d}\underline{X_{i}}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{j},\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{i},\mathrm{d}Y_{i}\right>}=\frac{L^{\circ}\left<\mathrm{d}X_{j},\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\right>}{L^{\circ}\left<\begin{array}[]{c}mX_{j}^{m-1}Y_{j}^{n}\mathrm{d}X_{j}+nX_{j}^{m}Y_{j}^{n-1}\mathrm{d}Y_{j},\\
m^{\prime}X_{j}^{m^{\prime}-1}Y_{j}^{n^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}X_{j}+n^{\prime}X_{j}^{m^{\prime}}Y_{j}^{n^{\prime}-1}\mathrm{d}Y_{j}\end{array}\right>},

which is a module of content

\displaystyle w\left(\det\begin{pmatrix}mX_{j}^{m-1}Y_{j}^{n}&nX_{j}^{m}Y_{j}^{n-1}\\
m^{\prime}X_{j}^{m^{\prime}-1}Y_{j}^{n^{\prime}}&n^{\prime}X_{j}^{m^{\prime}}Y_{j}^{n^{\prime}-1}\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=w((X_{j}Y_{j})^{-1}(X_{i}Y_{i})).

Just as in the case (
\gimel
) of the proof of
??
, we find that
\lim_{i\in\mathbb{N}}w(X_{i}Y_{i})=0
and
\lim_{j\in\mathbb{N}}w(X_{j}Y_{j})=0
.
Thus,

\lim_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\lim_{j\geq i}w((X_{j}Y_{j})^{-1}(X_{i}Y_{i}))=0.

We now turn to the case (
\daleth
), i.e., when
\mu=\infty
.
We will show that (
3.2.k
) holds by showing that the system
(\Omega_{A_{i}/A})_{i\in I}
is actually an increasing union and applying
??
.

Indeed, let
\alpha,\beta\in I
with
v(\beta)\geq v(\alpha)
.
Then we easily verify that

\frac{A_{\alpha}[X_{\beta}]}{\beta\alpha^{-1}X_{\beta}-X_{\alpha}}\simeq A_{\beta},X_{\beta}\mapsto\beta^{-1}\varphi,

where
\beta\alpha^{-1}X_{\beta}-X_{\alpha}
is a regular sequence.
Thus,
\mathbb{L}_{A_{\beta}/A_{\alpha}}
is concentrated in degree
0
and
\Omega_{A_{\beta}/A_{\alpha}}
is finitely presented.
So the sequence

0\to\Omega_{A_{\alpha}/A}\otimes L^{\circ}\to\Omega_{A_{\beta}/A}\otimes L^{\circ}\to\Omega_{A_{\beta}/A_{\alpha}}\otimes L^{\circ}\to 0,

is exact, and the first map is injective.
Hence, the system
(\Omega_{A_{i}/A})_{i\in I}
is increasing.
∎

3.3
Inducting on MacLane-Vaquié chains

3.3.1

We are now at a point where we can interpret our work on enlargements and their differentials for augmentations of semi-valuations on
K[T]
.

??
is a straightforward summary of the results of the previous section in the specific case of an ordinary augmentation of a semi-valuation on
K[T]
.
Most of the technical work left is in
??
and
??
where we show that similar results hold for limit augmentations. This requires a limit argument using enlargements that is not entirely straightforward.
Finally, in
??
, we use distinguished triangles to apply this to augmentation chains.

3.3.2 Lemma
.

Let
w
be a semi-valuation on
K[T]
and let
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Let
S
be a multiplicative subset of
K[T]
such that
(S^{-1}K[T],w)
is scalable and let
A\to S^{-1}K[T]
be any ring morphism.
Then

\mathbb{L}_{(S^{-1}K[T],w)^{\circ}/A}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}/A}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}.

Proof.

The ring
K[T]_{\ker w}
is a localization of
S^{-1}K[T]
which is scalable.

??
shows that there is some multiplicative set
S^{\prime}\subset S^{-1}K[T]
such that
{S^{\prime}}^{-1}S^{-1}K[T]=K[T]_{\ker w}
and
w(S^{\prime})=\{0\}
.

Then

\mathbb{L}_{(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}/A}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq{S^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{(S^{-1}K[T],w)^{\circ}/A}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}

∎

3.3.3 Lemma

(
\mathbb{L}
for an ordinary augmentation)
.

Let
v\leq w
be two semi-valuations on
K[T]
, and let
S
be a multiplicative system of
w
-stable elements such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v)
is scalable.
Consider
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Let
\mathfrak{a}=[v,\varphi,\mu]
be a
w
-optimal ordinary augmentation and let
S^{\prime}
be the multiplicative system generated by
S
and
\varphi
if
\mu\neq\infty
and
S^{\prime}=S
if
\mu=\infty
.
Write
R_{1}=(S^{-1}K[T],v)^{\circ}
and
R_{2}=(S^{\prime-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])^{\circ}
.
Then,

(i)

R_{2}=[(S^{-1}K[T],v),\varphi,\mu]

(ii)

(S^{\prime-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])
is scalable and on
(S^{\prime-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])
it holds that
[\mathfrak{a}]\leq w
.

(iii)

\mathbb{L}_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]

(iv)

\cont\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{a}+\inf(\Gamma_{v,>0})-\inf(\Gamma_{[\mathfrak{a}],>0})

Proof.

These are straight forward consequences of previous lemmas.
(i) and (ii) follows from
??
. (iii) and (iv) follow from
??
.
∎

3.3.4 Lemma

(
\mathbb{L}
for a limit augmentation of finite log-radius)
.

Let
v\leq w
be two semi-valuations on
K[T]
and
S
a multiplicative system of
w
-stable elements such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v)
is scalable.
Let
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
be a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Suppose
\mathfrak{a}=(v,(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I},\varphi,\mu)
is a
w
-optimal limit augmentation with
\mu\neq\infty
.
Let
S^{\prime}\subset K[T]
be the multiplicative set generated by
S,\varphi
and all
\psi_{i},i\in I
.
For each
i\in I
, write
S_{i}
for the multiplicative set generated by
S
and all
\psi_{j}
with
j\leq i
, and
v_{i}=[v,\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]
.
We write
R_{1}:=(S^{-1}K[T],v)^{\circ}
and
R_{2}:=({S^{\prime}}^{-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])^{\circ}
.

(i)

There is an increasing union

R_{2}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ},\varphi,\mu\right].

(ii)

(S^{\prime-1}K[T],v^{\prime})
is scalable and
S^{\prime}
is
w
-stable.

(iii)

\mathbb{L}_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}=\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]

(iv)

\cont\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{a}
.
Note that in this case,
\Gamma_{v}
is necessarily dense, so the fudge terms
\inf(\Gamma_{v,>0})-\inf(\Gamma_{[\mathfrak{a}],>0})
vanish automatically.

Proof.

Unlike the previous lemma, there is still an approximation argument needed to take care of the limit augmentation.

(i)

Let
a\in R_{2}
be any element.
Define
S^{\prime\prime}:=\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
.
Then we can write
a=\frac{b}{s}\varphi^{-n}
with
s\in S^{\prime\prime},b\in K[T],n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
v^{\prime}(b)\geq v^{\prime}(s)-n\mu
and write
\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}b_{\ell}\varphi^{\ell}
for the
\varphi
-expansion of
b
.
Because
\deg b_{\ell}<\deg\varphi
and
\varphi
is a limit-key polynomial, we know that
(v_{i}(b_{\ell}))_{i\in I}
stabilizes.
Choose
j\in I
sufficiently large, such that
v_{i}(b_{\ell})
has stabilized for all
\ell=0,\ldots,n
and
s\in S_{j}
.
Then
v^{\prime}(s)=v_{j}(s)
because all valuations of the generators of
S_{j}
have stabilized.
Also,

v^{\prime}(a)=\min_{\ell=0}^{n}\{v_{j}(s^{-1}b_{\ell})+(\ell-n)\cdot\mu\}\geq 0,\text{ thus }v_{j}(s^{-1}b_{\ell})+(\ell-n)\cdot\mu\geq 0,\forall\ell

So we can write each term
s^{-1}b_{\ell}\varphi^{m-n}=\alpha_{\ell}\cdot s^{-1}\beta_{\ell}^{-1}\varphi^{m-n}
with
\beta_{\ell}\in\vd_{v_{j}(b_{\ell})}^{w}
and
b_{\ell}=\beta_{\ell}^{-1}\alpha_{\ell}
and
a\in\left[(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j}),\varphi,\mu\right]
.
So
s^{-1}b_{\ell}\varphi^{m-n}\in[(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j}),\varphi,\mu]
and thus
a\in[(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j}),\varphi,\mu]
.

By definition

[(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j}),\varphi,\mu]=(S_{j}^{-1},K[T])^{\circ}[a^{-1}\varphi^{n}\mid a\in\vd(S_{j}^{-1}K[T]),n\in\mathbb{Z},n\cdot\mu-v(a)\geq 0],

and, as
v^{\prime}\geq v_{j}
we see that each
a^{-1}\varphi\in R_{2}
.

(ii)

The value group
\Gamma_{v}^{\prime}
is generated by
\Gamma_{v},\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I},\mu
.
So any
r\in\Gamma_{v}^{\prime}
can be written as a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear combination
r=r_{v}+\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}\gamma_{i}+b\cdot\mu
with
a_{i}=0
for all but finitely many
i\in I
and
r_{v}=v(\alpha),\alpha\in\vd_{w}((S^{-1}K[T],v))
.
Then
\alpha\cdot\varphi^{b}\cdot\prod_{i\in I}\psi_{i}^{a_{i}}
is a constant with valuation
r
.

(iii-iv)

Consider the tower of rings
R_{1}\to(S^{-1}_{i}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}\to\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]
. Note that up to a localization of the middle ring, this is actually two consecutive enlargements. Consider the associated triangle of cotangent complexes

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{(S^{-1}_{i}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{R_{1}}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{(S^{-1}_{i}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}}}.

By
??
we know that
(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}
is a localization of
(S^{-1}\psi^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}=[S^{-1}K[T],\psi,\gamma_{i}]
by some multiplicative system
T
, consisting of
w
-stable elements of valuation
0
which are units in
L^{\circ}
.

Thus, taking
-\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}
gives the triangle

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{[S^{-1}K[T],\psi,\gamma_{i}]}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{(S^{-1}_{i}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}.

??
,
??
show that the outer complexes are concentrated in degree
0
, thus so is the middle complex, i.e.

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{R_{1}}}\otimes L^{\circ}[0].

And by the additivity of content and
??
,
??
we find

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{R_{1}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{[S^{-1}K[T],\psi,\gamma_{i}]}{R_{1}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)+\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{\left[(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i}),\varphi,\mu\right]}{(S^{-1}_{i}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{i}-v(\psi_{i})+\mu-v_{i}(\varphi).

Then (iii) follows from the fact that tensor products and differentials commute with filtered colimits.
Statement (iv) would follow if we can show that content commutes with the colimit in this case.
We will give a similar argument as in the proof of
??
.
Recall that limit augmentations only occur when
K
is not discretely valued.
So all semi-valuations involved have dense value group.

(
\aleph
)

Case
\mu\in\Gamma_{v}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
:

For each
i\in I
let
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
be the minimal integer such that
m_{i}\cdot\mu\in\Gamma_{i}
.
Because
\Gamma_{i}
increases with
i
, we see that
m_{i}
decreases, and thus must stabilize at some point to
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>1}
.
Let
k\in I
be an element where
m_{k}=m
and let
I^{\prime}=\{j\in I\mid j\geq k\}
.
We can also take
\alpha\in\vd(S_{k}^{-1}K[T],v_{k})
such that
v_{k}(\alpha)=m\cdot\mu
.
Note that
\alpha
satisfies the same properties for all
j\in I^{\prime}
.

Write
A_{i}=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})
for all
i\in I^{\prime}
.
Then we can write

[A_{i}^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]=\bigcup_{j\in J_{i}}A_{(i,j)},

with the poset
J_{i}
and the ring
A_{(i,j)}
as in
??
, and uniformly used
\alpha
as the constant with value
\mu\cdot m
.

Define
\vd(\mathcal{A})=\bigcup_{i\in I^{\prime}}\vd(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})
.
Note that this is an increasing union.
Then we define

J=\{(i,(\beta,1))\in I^{\prime}\times(\vd(\mathcal{A})\times\mathbb{Z})\mid(\beta,1)\in J_{i}\},

which is a directed poset where
(j,(\gamma,1))\geq(i,(\beta,1))\iff w(\gamma)\geq w(\beta)\wedge j\geq i
.
Note that
w(\beta)\to\mu
as
(i,(\beta,1))
increases.

Then we easily verify that
R_{2}=\bigcup_{(i,(\beta,1))\in J}A_{i,(\beta,1)}
.
We will argue that
(\Omega_{A_{i,(\beta,1)}/R_{1}})_{(i,(\beta,1))\in J}
satisfies condition (c) from
??
.
Let
(j,(\gamma,1))\geq(i,(\beta,1))
then the mapping
X\mapsto X,Z\mapsto Z,Y_{(\beta,1)}\mapsto\beta^{-1}\gamma Y_{\gamma,1}
induces the following commutative diagram:

Then exactly the same argument as in the proof of
??
shows that

\cont\left(\coker\Omega_{A_{i,(\beta,1)}/R_{1}}\to\Omega_{A_{j,(\gamma,1)}/R_{1}}\right)\leq\mu-w(\beta)

and, that condition (c) of
??
holds.

(
\beth
)

Case
\mu\not\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}(\infty)
:

Again we write
A_{i}=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})
and

[A_{i}^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]=\bigcup_{j\in J_{i}}A_{(i,j)},

as in
??
.

Let

J=\{(i,(\alpha,\beta))\in I^{\prime}\times(\vd(\mathcal{A})^{2}\times\mathbb{Z})\mid(\alpha,\beta)\in J_{i}\},

be the directed poset with
(j,(\gamma,\delta))\geq(i,(\alpha,\beta))\iff w(\gamma)\geq w(\alpha)\wedge w(\delta)\geq w(\beta)\wedge j\geq i
.

R_{2}=\bigcup_{(i,j)\in J}A_{(i,j)}
.
Now let
(j,(\gamma,\delta))\geq(i,(\alpha,\beta))
and consider the mapping

yields the following commutative diagram:

3.3.5 Lemma

(
\mathbb{L}
for stable continuous family or almost stable limit augmentation)
.

Let
v\leq w
be two semi-valuations on
K[T]
and
S
a multiplicative system of
w
-stable elements such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v)
is scalable.
Let
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
be a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Suppose
\mathfrak{a}=(v,(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I})
is a
w
-optimal stable continuous family or
\mathfrak{a}=(v,(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I},\varphi,\mu)
is a
w
-optimal almost stable limit augmentation.
Let
S^{\prime}\subset K[T]
be the multiplicative set generated by
S
and all
\psi_{i},i\in I
.
For each
i\in I
, write
S_{i}
for the multiplicative set generated by
S
and all
\psi_{j}
with
j\leq i
, and
v_{i}=[v,\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]
.
We write
R_{1}:=(S^{-1}K[T],v)^{\circ}
and
R_{2}:=({S^{\prime}}^{-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])^{\circ}
.

(i)

There is an increasing union

R_{2}=\bigcup_{i\in I}(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}.

(ii)

(S^{\prime-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])
is scalable and
[\mathfrak{a}]=w
.

(iii)

\mathbb{L}_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}=\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]

(iv)

\cont\left(\Omega_{R_{2}/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{a}
.

Proof.

(i)

For an almost stable limit augmentation we know that
w=[\mathfrak{a}]=\lim_{i}v_{i}
where this is the point wise limit, and it stabilizes for every
f\in K[T]
with
v^{\prime}(f)<\infty
.
Let
f/s
be any element in
R_{2}
with
f\in K[T]
and
s\in S^{\prime}
.
Then there is some sufficiently large
j\in I
such that
s\in S_{j}
and
v_{j}(f)=w(f)
if
f\not\in\ker[\mathfrak{a}]
or
v_{j}(f)>v_{j}(s)
when
f\in\ker[\mathfrak{a}]
.
As
f/s\in R
we see that
[\mathfrak{a}](f)-[\mathfrak{a}](s)\geq 0
from which we can conclude that
f/s\in(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j})^{\circ}
.
The argument when
\mathfrak{a}
is a stable continuous family is the same.

(ii)

Let
r\in\Gamma_{w}
be any element.
Then there is some
j\in J
such that
r\in\Gamma_{v_{j}}
.
Then there is a constant
\alpha\in(S_{j}^{-1}K[T],v_{j})^{\times}
with
v_{j}(\alpha)=r
.
Thus,
\alpha
is a constant of
(S^{\prime-1}K[T],w)^{\times}
with
w(\alpha)=r
.

(iii-iv)

Write
A_{i}=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}
for every
i\in I
.
Then
A_{i}
is a localization of
[R_{1},\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]
by elements of value
0
, which map to invertible elements of
L^{\circ}
.
Thus,

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{A_{i}}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\frac{[R_{1},\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]}{R_{1}}}\simeq(\Omega_{\frac{A_{1}}{R_{1}}})[0],\quad\cont(\Omega_{\frac{A_{i}}{R_{1}}}\otimes L^{\circ})=\gamma_{i}-v(\psi_{i})+\inf(\Gamma_{v,>0})-\inf(\Gamma_{[v,\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}],>0}).

Similarly, for any
j>i
we see that
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{A_{i}}{R_{1}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq(\Omega_{\frac{A_{i}}{R_{1}}})[0]
and thus that the system
(\Omega_{A_{i}/R_{1}}\otimes L^{\circ})_{i\in I}
is an increasing union.
We conclude by taking the filtered colimits over
i
.

∎

3.3.6 Theorem
.

Let
w
be any semi-valuation on
K[T]
and let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain starting in a simple Gauss semi-valuation
v_{0}
.
Consider
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Let
S_{0}
be a multiplicative set of
w
-stable elements such that
(S_{0}^{-1}K[T],v_{0})
is scalable.
Further, let
S_{i}
be the multiplicative set generated by all key polynomials
\varphi_{j},\psi_{j,k}
for all
j\leq i,k\in J_{j}
, and let
S
be the multiplicative system generated by all
S_{i}
.
Let
R_{i}=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}
and
R_{w}=(S^{-1}K[T],w)^{\circ}

Then for each
i\in[0,n]\cap\mathbb{Z}
we have

(i)

\mathbb{L}_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{n}}L^{\circ}=\left(\Omega_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{i}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]
,

(ii)

\cont\left(\Omega_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{i}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{i}+\inf(\Gamma_{v_{0},>0})-\inf(\Gamma_{v_{i},>0})
,

(iii)

R_{i}
is scalable and
R_{w}
is scalable.

(iv)

\mathbb{L}_{R_{w}/R_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}/R_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\Omega_{R_{w}/R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]
,

(v)

\cont\left(\Omega_{R_{w}/R_{0}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)=\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}}{R_{0}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{n}+\inf(\Gamma_{v_{0},>0})-\inf(\Gamma_{w,>0})
.

Proof.

We may assume that none of the augmentations in the chain are limit augmentations that are not almost stable and have log-radius
\infty
.
Suppose that there is such an augmentation then necessarily the chain is finite and the only such augmentation is the last augmentation
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=(v_{n-1},(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i},\varphi_{n},\infty)
.

Then we can split this limit augmentation up into one limit augmentation with finite log-radius and one ordinary augmentation with infinite log-radius.
Indeed, let
\alpha\in K^{\circ}
with
\mu_{n}^{\prime}=v(\alpha)
be such that
\lim_{i}[v_{n-1},\psi_{i},\gamma](\varphi_{n})<\mu_{n}^{\prime}<\infty
.
Write
\varphi_{n}^{\prime}=\varphi_{n}+\alpha
.
Then
\varphi^{\prime}
is also a limit key polynomial of
(v_{n-1},(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}))
.
Let
\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{\prime}=(v_{n-1},(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i},\varphi_{n}^{\prime},\mu_{n}^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}^{\prime}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{\prime}],\varphi_{n},\infty)
.
Then the chain
(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{a}_{n-1},\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{\prime},\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}^{\prime})
is also a
w
-optimal augmentation chain approximating
w
, and containing no limit augmentations of infinite log-radius.
We write
\mathfrak{A}=(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}=(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{a}_{n-1},\mathfrak{a}_{n}^{\prime},\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}^{\prime})
.

Suppose that the theorem holds for the chain
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
.
Then, (i), (ii), and (iii) are equivalent to the theorem for
\mathfrak{A}
when
i<n
.
When
i=n
, these are equivalent to (iv) and (v) which are equivalent for both
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
by
??
.

From now on, we assume that there are no non-almost-stable limit augmentations of infinite log-radius in
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
.
We will first show (i), (ii), (iii) using induction and then obtain (iv) and (v) by taking colimits.
Clearly
\mathbb{L}_{R_{0}/R_{0}}=0
and
\Omega_{R_{0}/R_{0}}=0
, thus the base case is trivial.
Suppose that (i), (ii) hold for
i
, then consider the tower of rings
R_{0}\to R_{i}\to R_{i+1}
and the corresponding distinguished triangle of cotangent complexes tensored by
L^{\circ}
:

\mathbb{L}_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{n}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{R_{i+1}/R_{0}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{i+1}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{R_{i+1}/R_{i}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{i+1}}L^{\circ}.

From
??
,
??
and the induction hypothesis we see that the outer complexes are concentrated in degree
0
, so we obtain (i). We conclude (ii) and (iii) from the same lemmas and the additivity of content.
From this triangle we also see that the map
\Omega_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes L^{\circ}\to\Omega_{R_{i+1}/R_{0}}\otimes L^{\circ}
is injective.

If
n
is finite, then
R_{w}=R_{n}
thus (iv) and (v) hold immediately.
If
n=\infty
, then we obtain
R_{w}=\bigcup_{i\in I}R_{i}
, which is an increasing union, and as we’ve just argued
(\Omega_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes L^{\circ})_{i=1}^{\infty}
is an increasing union too.
Then we obtain (iv) and (v) by taking colimits as before.
∎

3.3.7 Remark
.

We continue with the notation as in
??
.
When
K
is discretely valued, we know that no limit augmentations occur.
Suppose that the augmentation chain
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
is finite and only consists of ordinary augmentations with log-radius in
\Gamma_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.
In the Berkovich setting, this is equivalent to
w
being a type II point.
Then each
R_{i+1}
is an enlargement of
R_{i}
of type (
\aleph
.d) and thus by
??
we know that
R_{i+1}
is complete intersection over
R_{i}
given by three variables
[X_{i},Y_{i},Z_{i}]
and relations
X_{i}Z_{i}-1,f_{i},g_{i}
as in
??
.
Note that
Y_{i}
maps to a constant of minimal valuation in
R_{i+1}
so
Y_{i}
can take the role of
\pi
in
??
when finding the presentation of
R_{i+2}
as an
R_{i+1}
algebra.

So we can find an explicit presentation of
R_{n}
as a complete intersection over
R_{0}
as

R_{n}=\frac{R_{0}[X_{0},Y_{0},Z_{0},\ldots,X_{n-1},Y_{n-1},Z_{n-1}]}{(X_{0}Z_{0}-1,f_{0},g_{0},\ldots,X_{n-1}Y_{n-1}-1,f_{n-1},g_{n-1})}.

It might also be possible to compute other invariants like the precise structure of
\Omega_{R_{n}/R_{0}}
and not only its content.

3.4
The log cotangent complex of an enlargement

3.4.1

Using the previous computation we may also compute the log differentials and more generally the log cotangent complex of enlargements.
Recall that a pre-log structure on a ring
A
is the data of a monoid
M
and a morphism of monoids
\alpha:M\to(A,\cdot)
.
A ring equipped with a pre-log structure is called a pre-log ring, and we will write it as
(M\xrightarrow{\alpha}A)
. Some sources may write this as
(A,M)
.
A morphism of pre-log rings
f:(M_{A}\to A)\to(M_{B}\to B)
is the data of a map of monoids
f_{M}:M_{A}\to M_{B}
and a map of rings
f_{R}:A\to B
such that the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{R}}

We say that the pre-log structure or pre-log ring
(M\xrightarrow{\alpha}A)
is a log structure or log ring, respectively, if
\alpha
induces an isomorphism
\alpha^{-1}(A^{\times})\to A^{\times}
.
Any pre-log ring or pre-log structure can be turned into a log ring or log structure in a canonical way, called logification, which is left adjoint to the inclusion of the category of log rings to the category of pre-log rings, see
[
16
, Chapter II, Proposition 1.1.5]
.
Intuitively, one can think of logification as adjoining all units of
A
to
M
.

3.4.2

For a morphism of pre-log rings
(M_{A}\to A)\to(M_{B}\xrightarrow{\beta}B)
, the module
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{((M_{B})\to B)/(M_{A}\to A)}
is the logarithmic analogue of the ordinary differentials.
It is the universal log derivation, meaning that it has a differential
\mathrm{d}:B\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{B}\to B)/(M_{A}\to A)}
and the log differential map
\dlog:M_{B}\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{B}\to B)/(M_{A}\to A)}
which satisfies
\mathrm{d}\beta(m)=\beta(m)\cdot\dlog m
for every
m\in M_{B}
.

Let
(M_{A}\to A)\to(M_{B}\to B)\to(M_{C}\to C)
be a tower of pre-log rings.
Analogous to ordinary differentials, there is an exact sequence

\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{B}\to B)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\otimes_{B}C\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{C}\to C)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{C}\to C)}{(M_{B}\to B)}}\to 0,

and one may wonder whether this sequence can be extended to the left.
Analogous to the cotangent complex, the log cotangent complex provides an answer to this question.
In this text, the log cotangent will always mean Gabber’s log cotangent complex.
The alternative, Olsson’s log cotangent complex, is unsuitable for our purposes as it is only defined for fs log rings.
Gabber’s log cotangent complex is described in
[
17
, § 8]
, and together with
[
5
, § 2]
it gives a good description of how to use it in practice.
For any tower of pre-log rings as above, there is a distinguished triangle

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{B}\to B)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{B}C\to\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{C}\to C)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\to\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{C}\to C)}{(M_{B}\to B)}}.

The log cotangent complex is also compatible with the ordinary cotangent complex in the sense that for any ring morphism
A\to B
and pre-log structure
M\to A
there are natural quasi-isomorphisms

\mathbb{L}_{\scriptstyle\frac{B}{A}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(0\to B)}{(0\to A)}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{M\to B}{M\to A}},

(3.4.a)

see
[
17
, Lemma 8.21, Lemma 8.22]
.
The log cotangent complex is also invariant under logification, in the sense that if two morphisms
(M_{A}\to A)\to(M_{B}\to B)
and
(M_{A}^{\prime}\to A)\to(M_{B}^{\prime}\to B)
logify to the same morphism then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{B}\to B)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\scriptstyle\frac{(M_{B}^{\prime}\to B)}{(M_{A}^{\prime}\to A)}},

see
[
17
, Lemma 8.20]
.

3.4.3

Let
(A,v)
be a semi-valued
K
-algebra without zero divisors.
The
total log structure on
A^{\circ}
is the inclusion of monoids
\vd(A^{\circ})\hookrightarrow A^{\circ}
.
Also note that if
A=L
is a field, then the total log structure on
L^{\circ}
is
L^{\circ}\setminus\{0\}
and thus agrees with the usual notion of the total or integral log structure.

If
\ker v\subset A
is a principal ideal generated by
f
, then the
boundary log structure
is defined as
\bls(A^{\circ})=(A[f^{-1}])^{\times}\cap A^{\circ}
.

3.4.4

Let
(A,v_{A})\to(B,v_{B})
be a map of scalable
K
-algebras and let
M_{B}\to B^{\circ}
be the total or boundary log structure if that is well-defined.
Let
\varphi,\psi\in B^{\circ}
be two elements that only differ by a multiplication by a constant in
A
, say without loss of generality
\psi=\alpha\varphi
with
\alpha\in\const A^{\circ}
.
Then as elements of
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{B}\to B^{\circ})/(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}
we see that
\dlog\psi=\dlog\alpha+\dlog\varphi=\dlog\varphi
.
So by abuse of notation, for
\varphi\in B
such that there is a
\theta\in\const A^{\circ}
such that
\theta\cdot\varphi\in M_{B}
we will write
\dlog\varphi=\dlog\theta\varphi
.

3.4.5

Let
(A,v_{A})\to(B,v_{B})
be a map of scalable
K
-algebras.
Suppose that
\Gamma_{A}=\Gamma_{B}
, then it is easily verified that
(\const A\to B)
logifies to
(\const B\to B)
.
Then

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const B\to B)}{(\const A\to A)}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A\to B)}{(\const A\to A)}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{B}{A}}.

3.4.6 Lemma
.

Let
(M_{A}\xrightarrow{\alpha}A)
be a pre-log ring and let
a\in A
be any element.
Consider the pre-log ring
(M_{A}\oplus\mathbb{N}\to A)
where the monoid map is given by
(m,n)\mapsto\alpha(m)\cdot a^{n}
.
Then

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{A}\oplus\mathbb{N}\to A)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\simeq(A\cdot\mathrm{d}a\to A\cdot\dlog a)[-1,0].

In particular, when
a
is not a zero divisor, this is quasi-isomorphic to
(\frac{A}{a\cdot A}\cdot\dlog a)[0]
.

Proof.

Consider the pre-log ring
(M_{A}\oplus\mathbb{N}\to A[T])
with the monoid map given by
(m,n)\mapsto\alpha(m)\cdot T^{n}
.
Note that
A\simeq A[T]/(T-a)
and that
T-a
is clearly a regular sequence in
A[T]
.
By
[
5
, Proposition 2.7]
we see that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{A}\oplus\mathbb{N}\to A)}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\simeq\left(\frac{(T-a)}{(T-a)^{2}}\xrightarrow{-\mathrm{d}}\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{A}\oplus\mathbb{N}\to A[T])}{(M_{A}\to A)}}\otimes A\right)[-1,0].

By
[
5
, Proposition 2.6]
the module in degree
0
is
A\cdot\dlog a
.
As
T-a
is regular,
(T-a)/(T-a)^{2}
is a rank 1 free
A
-module and its generator
(T-a)
is mapped to
-\mathrm{d}a
.
So we may identify it with
A\cdot\mathrm{d}a
.
∎

3.4.7 Proposition
.

Let
(A,v)
be a scalable
K
-algebra with
\ker v=\{0\}
and let
w\geq v
be another semi-valuation on
A
.
Let
\varphi\in A
be a prime element, and let
\mu=w(\varphi)
.
Consider the enlargement
[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]
, and let
M_{A,\varphi,\mu}
be the submodule generated by
\vd(A^{\circ})
and
\alpha^{-1}\varphi^{m}
with
n\cdot\mu-v(\alpha)\geq 0
.
Then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu])}{(\vd(A^{\circ})\to A^{\circ})}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left([A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}{(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}\cdot\dlog\varphi\right)[0].

Moreover, if the semi-valuation
v^{\prime}
from
??
is well-defined, then
M_{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}
is the total log structure on
([A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu],v^{\prime})
when
\mu\neq\infty
and the boundary log structure on when
\mu=\infty
. Recall that
[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]
equals
(A,v^{\prime})^{\circ}
when
\mu=\infty
and
(A[\varphi^{-1}],v^{\prime})^{\circ}
when
\mu<\infty
.

Proof.

By
??
, we know that
[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]
is the increasing union of a filtered system
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
of
A^{\circ}
-algebras.
Each is
A_{i}
has an explicit presentation as
A_{i}\simeq A^{\circ}[\underline{X_{i,j}}]/(\underline{f_{i,j}})
, where the relations
\underline{f_{i,j}}
form a regular sequence, see
??
.
Consider the pre-log structure
M_{i}\to A_{i}
where

M_{i}=(\vd(A)\oplus\varphi^{\mathbb{Z}})\cap A_{i}=\{\alpha^{-1}\varphi^{n}\mid\alpha\in\vd(A),n\in\mathbb{Z},n\cdot\mu-v(\alpha)\geq 0\}.

Define
M_{0}:=\const(A^{\circ})\oplus(\theta^{-1}\varphi)^{\mathbb{N}}
which is a log structure on
A^{\circ}
.

Note that the relations
f_{i,j}
from
??
identify elements from the monoid
M_{0}\oplus\underline{X_{i,j}}^{\mathbb{N}}
.
We write
\underline{F_{i,j}}
for the corresponding relations on
\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\oplus\underline{X_{i,j}}^{\mathbb{N}}\}.
2
2

2

We use the notation
R\{M\}
for a monoid ring and reserve square brackets for adjoining free variables or elements of a larger ring in which
R
is contained.
For example, here
A_{i}=A^{\circ}[M_{i}]
but this is not the monoid ring
A^{\circ}\{M_{i}\}
.

The exact same arguments as in
??
, and
??
show that
\mathbb{Z}\{M_{i}\}\simeq\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\oplus\underline{X_{i,j}}^{\mathbb{N}}\}/(\underline{F_{i,j}})
and that
(\underline{F_{i,j}})
is a regular sequence in
\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\oplus\underline{X_{i,j}}^{\mathbb{N}}\}
.
Now we see that

\displaystyle A^{\circ}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\}}\mathbb{Z}\{M_{i}\}

\displaystyle\simeq A^{\circ}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\}}K_{\bullet}(\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\oplus\underline{X_{i,j}}\},\underline{F_{i,j}})

\displaystyle=K_{\bullet}(A^{\circ}[\underline{X_{i,j}}],\underline{f_{i,j}})

\displaystyle\simeq A_{i}.

Here
K_{\bullet}
denotes the Kozul complex, and we use the well known fact that the Kozul complex associated to a regular sequence is a free resolution.
This shows that the diagram

is a homotopy push-out. Moreover, the pre-log structures
M_{0},M_{i}
are integral, thus by
[
5
, Corollary 2.4, Lemma 2.3]
the corresponding diagram of pre-log rings is a homotopy pushout.
Then

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{i}\to A_{i})}{(M_{0}\to A^{\circ})}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{i}\to\mathbb{Z}\{M_{i}\})}{(M_{0}\to\mathbb{Z}\{M_{0}\})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}\{M_{i}\}}A_{i}\simeq\frac{M_{i}^{\text{gp}}}{M_{0}^{\text{gp}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}A_{i}=0,

where the first quasi-isomorphism is due to Lemma 2.2 of loc. cit., the second is due to
[
17
, Lemma 8.23.(ii)]
and the last equality holds because both
M_{i}
and
M_{0}
groupify to
\vd(A)\oplus\varphi^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

By
[
5
, Proposition 2.9]
the log cotangent complex commutes with filtered colimits, thus by taking the colimit of the complex above we see that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu])}{(M_{0}\to A^{\circ})}}\simeq 0.

Consider the tower of pre-log rings
(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})\to(M_{0}\to A^{\circ})\to(M^{w}_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu])
.
Then, by the distinguished triangle and the vanishing above, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M^{w}_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu])}{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{0}\to A^{\circ})}{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]

\displaystyle\simeq\left([A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\right),

where the last quasi-isomorphism is due to
??
.

We still have to show that the log structures are as claimed when
v^{\prime}
is well-defined.
Note that
v^{\prime}(\alpha^{-1}\varphi^{n})=\mu\cdot n-v(\alpha)
, with
\alpha\in A^{\times}
.
Then we easily verify that
M_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}=A[\varphi^{-1}]^{\times}\cap(A[\varphi^{-1}],v^{\prime})^{\circ}
if
\mu<\infty
, and
M_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu}=A[\varphi^{-1}]^{\times}\cap(A,v^{\prime})^{\circ}
when
\mu=\infty
.
∎

3.4.8 Remark
.

Continue with the context of
??
.
The statement leaves open what the log cotangent complex of
[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]/A^{\circ}
is when
v^{\prime}
is well-defined,
\mu
is infinite, and we take the total log structure on
[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]
.
This easily reduces to the non-log case.
Indeed, in this case
\const A^{\circ}=\const(A,v^{\prime})^{\circ}
.
Both are the constants of
A^{\times}
, with positive valuation with respect to
v,v^{\prime}
respectively.
But the constants are
v^{\prime}
stable, see
??
, hence both sets are identical.
Then as in (
3.4.a
), we see that there is a quasi-isomorphism of complexes

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A^{\circ}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\infty])}{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\infty]}{A^{\circ}}},

and the latter is described in
??
after tensoring with a suitable field
L^{\circ}
.

3.4.9 Corollary
.

Continue with the context of
??
and suppose that
\mu=\infty
.
Let
(M_{B}\to B)\to(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})
be any morphism of log rings such that
H_{1}(\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}{(M_{B}\to B)}})=0

Then

\displaystyle\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ}/(\theta^{-1}\varphi))}{(M_{B}\to B)}}\otimes[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{A^{\circ},\varphi,\infty}\to[A^{\circ},\varphi,\infty])}{(M_{B}\oplus\Phi^{\mathbb{N}}\to B[\Phi])}},

where
\Phi
is a free variable mapping to
\theta^{-1}\varphi
.

Proof.

For notational clarity, we omit writing the log structures in the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}{(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{A^{\circ}}{B}}\otimes[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}{A^{\circ}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{A^{\circ}/(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}{B}}\otimes[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{[A^{\circ},\varphi,\mu]}{B[\Phi]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

The first two rows are is exact and each column is short exact.
By the snake lemma, the third row is also exact.
∎

3.5
The log cotangent complex for pure transcendental extensions

3.5.1 Lemma
.

Let
v\leq w
be two semi-valuations on
K[T]
, and let
S
be a multiplicative system of
w
-stable elements such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v)
is scalable.
Consider
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Let
\mathfrak{a}=[v,\varphi,\mu]
be a
w
-optimal ordinary augmentation and let
S^{\prime}
be the multiplicative system generated by
S
and
\varphi
if
\mu\neq\infty
and
S^{\prime}=S
if
\mu=\infty
.
Write
R_{1}=(S^{-1}K[T],v)^{\circ}
and
R_{2}=(S^{\prime-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])^{\circ}
.
Let
\theta\in\const(S^{-1}K[T],v)
with
v(\theta)=v(\varphi)
.

•

If
\mu\neq\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\simeq\left(R_{2}\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\to R_{2}\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\right)\simeq\left(\frac{R_{2}}{\theta^{-1}\varphi}\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\right)[0]

Thus
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})/(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}
is a cyclic module of content
\step\mathfrak{a}
.

•

If
\mu=\infty
, then for the boundary log structure it is the case that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\bls R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(L^{\circ}\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\xrightarrow{0}L^{\circ}\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi)\right)[-1,0]

whose zeroth and first homology are isomorphic to
L^{\circ}
.
For the total log structure, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{2}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\frac{L\cdot\mathrm{d}\varphi}{L^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi)}\right)[0].

3.5.2 Remark
.

When
\mu=\infty
we see that for both the boundary log structure and the total log structure, the zeroth homology of the log cotangent complex has infinite content.
However, the former is a free indivisible module, whereas the latter is a divisible torsion module.

Proof of
??
.

The case where
\mu\neq\infty
and the result for the boundary log structure when
\mu=\infty
follow immediately from
??
.
The if
\mu=\infty
and we consider the total log structure on
R_{2}
, then we know from
??
that this is the same as the non-log case.
The result now follows from tensoring (
3.2.i
) with
L^{\circ}
and taking the colimit.
∎

The proof of the analogous statement for limit augmentations in the logarithmic case is fortunately a bit easier than in the non-log case.

3.5.3 Lemma
.

Let
v\leq w
be two semi-valuations on
K[T]
and
S
a multiplicative system of
w
-stable elements such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v)
is scalable.
Let
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
be a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Suppose
\mathfrak{a}=(v,(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I},\varphi,\mu)
is a
w
-optimal limit augmentation.
Let
S^{\prime}\subset K[T]
be the multiplicative set generated by
S
, all
\psi_{i},i\in I
, and
\varphi
when
\mu\neq\infty
.
For each
i\in I
, write
S_{i}
for the multiplicative set generated by
S
and all
\psi_{j}
with
j\leq i
, and
v_{i}=[v,\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]
.

•

If
\mu\neq\infty
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)[0]\text{ and }\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{a}.

•

When
\mu=\infty
then

\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\bls R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{a}=\infty.

Proof.

We write
R_{1}:=(S^{-1}K[T],v)^{\circ}
and
R_{2}:=({S^{\prime}}^{-1}K[T],[\mathfrak{a}])^{\circ}
, and let
M_{B}
be the total log structure or boundary log structure on
R_{2}
when
\mu<\infty
or
\mu=\infty
respectively.
For each
i\in I
we write
v_{i}=[v,\psi,\gamma_{i}]
,
A_{i}:=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}
,
A_{i}^{\prime}:=[S^{-1}K[T],\psi_{i},\gamma_{i}]
,
B_{i}=[A_{i},\varphi,\mu]
and
A=\bigcup_{i\in I}A_{i}
.
Moreover, for each
i\in I
we choose a constant
\theta_{i}\in(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})
with
w(\theta)=v_{i}(\varphi)
and a constant
\lambda\in(S^{-1}K[T],v)
with
w(\lambda)=v(\psi_{i})
, which is independent of
i
by
??
.
Finally, we write
M_{i}=(S^{-1}K[T])^{\times}\cap(S^{-1}K[T])\cap[A_{i},\varphi,\mu]
.
Like in the proof of
??
, we know that

R_{2}=\bigcup_{i\in I}B_{i},

is an increasing union and that for each
i\in I
,
A_{i}
is a localization of
A_{i}^{\prime}
by inverting elements which map to invertible elements of
L^{\circ}
.
Moreover,
(\const A_{i}\to A_{i})
is the logification of
(\const A_{i}^{\prime}\to A_{i})
.
Thus, by
??

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A_{i}\to A_{i})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\frac{L^{\circ}}{(\lambda^{-1}\psi_{i})}\dlog(\psi_{i})\right).

As for any
j\geq i
we know that
(v_{i},\psi_{j},\gamma_{j})
is a well-defined augmentation, by the same argument the tower
(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})\to(\const A_{i}\to A_{i})\to(\const A_{j}\to A_{j})

0\to\frac{L^{\circ}}{\lambda^{-1}\psi_{i}}\dlog\psi_{i}\hookrightarrow\frac{L^{\circ}}{\lambda^{-1}\psi_{j}}\dlog\psi_{j}\to\frac{L^{\circ}}{\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j}}\dlog\psi_{j}\to 0,

where
\kappa
is a constant of value
\gamma_{j}-\gamma_{i}
.
So we see that
\dlog\psi_{i}=\kappa_{ij}\dlog\psi_{j}
where
\kappa_{ij}\in L
with
v_{L}(\kappa_{ij})=\gamma_{j}-\gamma_{i}
.
More suggestively, we may write the middle module as
\frac{\kappa_{ij}^{-1}\cdot L^{\circ}\dlog\psi_{i}}{(\lambda^{-1}\psi_{i})}
.
So taking the colimit over
j
we see that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\const A\to A)}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\frac{L_{>\lim_{j}\gamma_{j}-\gamma_{i}}}{L_{\geq v_{i}-v(\lambda)}}\cdot\dlog\psi_{i}\right)[0],

which is a module of content
\lim_{i}\gamma_{j}-v(\lambda)=\step\mathfrak{b}
, where
\mathfrak{b}
is the continuous family
(v,\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I}
.
It is not finitely generated, but it is generated by all
\dlog\psi_{j}
.

Moreover, by
??
, we know that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{i}\to B_{i})}{(\const A_{i}\to A_{i})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\theta_{i}^{-1}L^{\circ}\cdot(\varphi\dlog(\varphi))\to L^{\circ}\dlog(\varphi)[-1,0].

Taking the colimits over
i
of this complex, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const A\to A)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(L_{>-\lim_{i}{v_{i}(\varphi)}}\cdot\varphi\dlog\varphi\to L^{\circ}\dlog\varphi\right)[-1,0]

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(\frac{L^{\circ}}{L_{>-\lim_{i}v_{i}(\varphi)+\mu}}\cdot\dlog\varphi\right)&\text{ if }\mu<\infty\\
\displaystyle\left(L_{>-\lim_{i}v_{i}(\varphi)}\xrightarrow{0}L^{\circ}\cdot\dlog\varphi\right)[-1,0]&\text{ if }\mu=\infty\end{cases}.

In both cases we see that the content of the zeroth homology is
\lim_{i}\mu-v_{i}(\varphi)
.
Then the distinguished triangle associated to the tower of log rings
(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})\to(\const A\to A)\to(M_{2}\to R_{2})
shows

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\const A\to A)}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(M_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}.

When
\mu<\infty
then
M_{2}
is the total log structure, the outer complexes are concentrated in degree
0
, thus so is the middle one.
By the additivity of content we see that

\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{2}\to R_{2})}{(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)=\step\mathfrak{b}+\lim_{i}\mu-v_{i}(\varphi)=\step\mathfrak{a}.

When
\mu=\infty
, the distinguished triangle shows that
L^{\circ}\cdot\dlog\varphi
is a quotient of
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{{(\bls R_{2}\to R_{2})/(\const R_{1}\to R_{1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}
, and thus it has infinite content.
∎

3.5.6 Lemma
.

Let
w
be a semi-valuation on
K[T]
and let
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
be a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.

S
be a multiplicative subset of

such that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\displaystyle\frac{(\const A^{\circ}\to A^{\circ})}{(M_{B}\to B)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\displaystyle\frac{(\const(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}\to(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ})}{(M_{B}\to B)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}.

Moreover, when

then

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same as the proof of

.

and

3.5.7 Theorem
.

Let
w
be any semi-valuation on
K[T]
extending
v_{K}
and let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain on
v_{0}
approximating
w
.
Consider
(K[T],w)\to(L,v_{L})
a map of semi-valued rings to a valued field
L
.
Let
S_{0}
be a multiplicative set of
w
-stable elements such that
(S_{0}^{-1}K[T],v_{0})
is scalable.
Further, let
S_{i}
be the multiplicative set generated by all key polynomials
\varphi_{j},\psi_{j,k}
for all
j\leq i,k\in J_{j}
, and let
S
be the multiplicative set generated by all
S_{i}
.
Let
R_{i}=(S_{i}^{-1}K[T],v_{i})^{\circ}
and
R_{w}=(S^{-1}K[T],w)^{\circ}

Then for each
i\in[0,n]\cap\mathbb{Z}
we have

(i)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{i}\to R_{i})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{i}}L^{\circ}=\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{i}\to R_{i})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{i}}L^{\circ}[0]
,

(ii)

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{i}\to R_{i})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{i}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{i}
.

We write
A_{w}:=(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}
, then

(iii)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{w}\to R_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A_{w}\to A_{w}}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{w}\to R_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)[0]\end{aligned}
,

(iv)

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{w}\to R_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)=\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const A_{w}\to A_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{n}
.

Moreover, when
\ker w\neq\{0\}
, then

(v)

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\bls R_{w}\to R_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)=\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\bls A_{w}\to A_{w})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\otimes_{R_{w}}L^{\circ}\right)=\infty
.

Proof.

The proof of (i)-(iv) is the same as
??
with the log cotangent complex instead of the cotangent complex.

For (v), note that - as argued at the start of the proof of
??
- we may assume that
n
is finite and that
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an ordinary or almost stable limit augmentation with log-radius equal to
\infty
.
Then we know that

\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\bls R_{n}\to R_{n})}{(\const R_{n-1}\to R_{n-1})}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)=\infty,

by
??
and
??
.
By the fundamental exact sequence, this is a quotient of
\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\bls R_{n}\to R_{n})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\right)
which also must have infinite content.
∎

4
Extensions of valued fields, (log) differents, and discrepancies

4.1
Finite extensions and their (log) differents

4.1.1

Let
(L,v_{L})/(K,v_{K})
be a finite extension of valued fields.
The
different
of
L/K
is defined as
\mathfrak{d}_{L/K}:=\cont(\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
and the
log different
is defined likewise as
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}:=\cont(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
, where both
K^{\circ}
and
L^{\circ}
are equipped with their respective total log structures.
The computations from the previous section allow us to give formulas for the (log) differents of finite extensions without much extra work.

Suppose that
L=K[T]/(f)
where
f
is some irreducible polynomial. We may assume
f
to be monic and have coefficients in
K^{\circ}
, by substituting
f(T)
for
\alpha^{\deg f}f(\alpha^{-1}T)
with
v(\alpha)
sufficiently large.
Then the valuation
v_{L}
induces a semi-valuation
w
on
K[T]
with kernel
(f)
.

Let
v_{0}
be the Gauss valuation with center
0
and log-radius
0
.
Then we may approximate
w
with a
w
-optimal augmentation chain
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
on
v_{0}
, see
??
.
Like in the beginning of the proof of
??
, we may assume that
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],f,\infty)
is an ordinary augmentation or an almost stable limit augmentation
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I},f,\infty)
.

4.1.2 Definition
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}=(v,(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I},\varphi,\infty)
be an almost stable limit augmentation.
Write
v(\psi)=v(\psi_{i})
, which we recall is independent of
i
, see
??
.
We define

p_{\mathfrak{a}}:[v(\psi),\infty]\to\mathcal{V}(K[T]):\lambda\mapsto\begin{cases}[v,\psi_{j},\lambda]&\text{if }\lambda\leq\gamma_{j}\\
[\mathfrak{a}]&\text{if }\lambda=\infty\end{cases},

where
\mathcal{V}(K[T])
is the set of all semi-valuations on
K[T]
that extend
v_{K}
.
Note that
p_{\mathfrak{a}}
is well-defined by
[
12
, Lemma 2.8]
.
Moreover, for any
f\in K[T]
, we define

|f|_{\mathfrak{a}}:[v(\psi),\infty]\to\mathbb{R}\infty,\lambda\mapsto(p_{\mathfrak{a}}(\lambda))(f).

4.1.3

Let
\mathfrak{a}
be an almost stable limit augmentation and let
f\in K[T]
be any polynomial.
Take any
b>v(\psi)
and
i\in I
such that
\gamma_{i}\geq b
.
Let
f=\sum_{k=0}^{m}a_{k}\psi_{i}^{k}
be the
\psi_{i}
expansion of
f
.
Then

|f|_{\mathfrak{a}}|_{[v(\psi),b)]}:\lambda\mapsto\min_{k=0}^{m}\{v(a_{k})+k\cdot\lambda\},

from which we easily see that
|f|_{\mathfrak{a}}
is a non-decreasing piecewise
\mathbb{Z}
-linear function with non-increasing slopes.
In particular, the slope of
|f|_{\mathfrak{a}}
stabilizes to a minimal value.

4.1.4 Proposition
.

With notation as above, if
L/K
is separable (i.e.
f^{\prime}\neq 0
) then

\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\simeq\left(\Omega_{\textstyle\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\right)[0],\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\simeq\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\right)[0].

If
L/K
is inseparable, then

\displaystyle H_{i}\left(\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\right)=H_{i}\left(\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\right)

\displaystyle=0,\quad i\geq 2,

and the first homology is torsion free of rank
1
.

Proof.

Continue with the notation of
??
.
By our choice of
v_{0}
we see that
R_{0}=K^{\circ}[T]
.
Note that
\const R_{0}=\const K^{\circ}
.
Then

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{0}}{K^{0}}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}{(\const K^{0}\to K^{\circ})}}\simeq\left(R_{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}T\right)[0].

Then by the distinguished triangle associated to the tower
K\to R_{0}\to R_{n}
, equipped with the total log structure when considered as log rings, thus we see that

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{n}}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ},\quad\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{(\const R_{n}\to R_{n})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{0})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}

are concentrated in degree
0
and the zeroth homology has rank
\geq 1
, recall
??
and
??
.
Note that
R_{n}\to L^{\circ}
is surjective with kernel the ideal
\mathcal{I}=(\theta^{-1}f\mid\theta\in\const R_{w})
.
Let
(\theta_{i})
be a sequence of constants with
v_{i}(\theta_{i})
increasing and going to
\infty
, let
\mathcal{I}_{i}=\theta_{i}^{-1}f\cdot R_{n}
.
Then this is an increasing sequence of ideals generated by regular elements
\theta_{i}^{-1}f
.
Thus,

\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{R_{n}/\mathcal{I}_{i}}{R_{n}}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{n}\to R_{n}/\mathcal{I}_{i})}{(\const R_{n}\to R_{n})}}\simeq\left(\frac{R_{n}}{(\theta_{i}^{-1}f)}\theta_{i}^{-1}f\right)[-1].

Taking the colimit over
i
we see that

\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{L^{\circ}}{R_{n}}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const R_{n}\to R_{n})}}\simeq\left(L\cdot f\right)[-1].

Take the tower of rings
K_{0}\to R_{n}\to L^{\circ}
and the associated long exact sequence

0\to H^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}})\to L\cdot f\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{\frac{R_{n}}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\to\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\to 0.

(4.1.a)

If
L/K
is separable, then
f^{\prime}\neq 0
and
\mathrm{d}f
does not vanish in
\Omega_{L/K}
.
Then
L\cdot f
is not torsion, thus
\mathrm{d}
is injective and thus
H_{1}(\mathbb{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{0}})
vanishes.

If
L/K
is inseparable, then
f^{\prime}=0
and
\mathrm{d}f
does vanish in
\Omega_{L/K}
.
So the morphism
\mathrm{d}
is not injective and therefore
H_{1}(\mathbb{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
is a non-trivial submodule of
L
, whence it is of the prescribed form.
The exact same argument holds for the log cotangent complex.
∎

4.1.5 Theorem
.

Continue with the notation as above.
When
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an ordinary augmentation, the (log)different equals,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{L/K}

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1}-v_{n-1}(f)+v_{n-1}(f^{\prime})+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{L,>0}\right),

(4.1.b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1}-v_{n-1}(f)+v_{n-1}(f^{\prime}).

When
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an almost stable limit augmentation on a continuous family
\mathfrak{b}=(\mathfrak{b}_{i})_{i\in I}
, then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{L/K}

\displaystyle=\lim_{i\in I}\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{i-1}+\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}-[\mathfrak{b}_{i}](f)+[\mathfrak{b}_{i}](f^{\prime})+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{L,>0}\right),

(4.1.c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}

\displaystyle=\lim_{i\in I}\step(\mathfrak{a}_{j})_{j=1}^{i-1}+\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}-[\mathfrak{b}_{i}](f)+[\mathfrak{b}_{i}](f^{\prime}).

Moreover, these limits stabilize.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we assume that log structure on the rings in the arguments of the log cotangent complex and log differentials are equipped with the total log structure.
Any quotients like
A/\mathcal{I}
we also equip with the log structure
\const A
.
Let
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}
when
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an ordinary augmentation and let
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{b}_{i}
for some
i\in I
when
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an almost stable limit augmentation.
Write
v^{\prime}=[\mathfrak{c}]
.

If
L/K
is inseparable, then we see from (
4.1.a
) that
\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
and
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
must be rank 1 modules.
Therefore, the (log)-different is
\infty
, and the right-hand sides are also
\infty
as
f^{\prime}=0
.

Assume that
L/K
is separable.
By
??
and
??
, there is a multiplicative system
S
such that
(S^{-1}K[T],v^{\prime})
is scalable, and the content of the (log)-differentials of
R^{\prime}/R_{0}
is given by these theorems, where
R^{\prime}=(S^{-1}K[T],v^{\prime})^{\circ},R_{0}=K^{\circ}[T]=(K[T],v_{0})^{\circ}
.
Let
\theta
be a constant in
(S^{-1},K[T],v^{\prime})
with
w(\theta)=v^{\prime}(f)
.
We will compute
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}
by understanding
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}
,
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}
, and
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}/(f)}}
. Then we can put this together using distinguished triangles. The same works for the log cotangent complexes.

•

Let’s focus our attention on
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}
and
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}
.
We know that
R_{0}=K^{\circ}[T]
and therefore
f\in R_{0}
is not a zero divisor and
f
is a regular sequence.
Hence,
\mathbb{L}{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\simeq\left((R_{0}/(f))\cdot f\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}}(R_{0}/(f))\mathrm{d}T\right)
in degree
-1
and
0
.
Applying
-\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{0}/(f)}L^{\circ}
shows that

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}(L^{\circ}\cdot f\xrightarrow{\cdot f^{\prime}}L^{\circ}\mathrm{d}T),\text{ and }\cont\left(\frac{L^{\circ}\mathrm{d}T}{L^{\circ}f^{\prime}\mathrm{d}T}\right)=w(f^{\prime})=v_{n-1}(f^{\prime}).

As
L/K
separable, the map
\mathrm{d}
is injective, and thus these complexes are concentrated in degree
0
.

•

Note that
R^{\prime}/(f\cdot R^{\prime})\simeq R^{\prime}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R_{0}}R_{0}/(f\cdot R_{0})
. So by
[
19
,
Tag 08QQ
]
, we have that
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R^{\prime}}\frac{R^{\prime}}{(f)}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}
.
Applying
-\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R^{\prime}/f}L^{\circ}
to both sides and simplifying, we see that
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R^{\prime}/(f)}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes_{R^{\prime}}L^{\circ}
.
So by
??
we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R^{\prime}/(f)}L^{\circ}

\displaystyle\simeq\Omega_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes L^{\circ}[0],

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{c}+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{v^{\prime},>0}\right).

Using
[
5
, Proposition 2.1, Lemmas 2.2-2.3]
, the same argument shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{R^{\prime}/(f)}L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes L^{\circ}[0],\quad\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{c}.

•

Finally, we turn our attention to
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}/(f)}}
.
Consider the tower
R^{\prime}\to R^{\prime}/(f)\to R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)
, and the corresponding triangle of cotangent complexes,

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R^{\prime}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}/(f)}}.

Note that the both
f
and
\theta^{-1}f
are regular sequences in
R^{\prime}
, therefore we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R^{\prime}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{R^{\prime}}}\simeq\left(f\cdot L^{\circ}\right)[-1],

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}}}\simeq\left(\theta^{-1}f\cdot L^{\circ}\right)[-1],

Filling this in the triangle above (and the logarithmic analogue), we see that

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}/(f)}}\simeq\frac{\theta^{-1}fL^{\circ}}{fL^{\circ}}[-1],\text{ and }\cont\left(\frac{\theta^{-1}f\cdot L^{\circ}}{f\cdot L^{\circ}}\right)=w(\theta)=v^{\prime}(f).

The tower of rings
K^{\circ}\to R_{0}/(f)\to R^{\prime}/(f)
gives the triangle

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{0}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{n-1}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R_{n-1}/(f)}{R_{0}/(f)}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}.

So we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\Omega_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)[0],

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\right)

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{c}+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{v^{\prime},>0}\right)+v^{\prime}(f).

The tower
K^{\circ}\to R^{\prime}\to R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)
shows that
\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}
is concentrated in degree
0
.
Next, consider the triangle corresponding to the tower
K^{\circ}\to R^{\prime}/(f)\to R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)
,

\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{R^{\prime}/(f)}}.

The corresponding LES reads

\ldots\to 0\to\frac{\theta^{-1}f\cdot L^{\circ}}{f\cdot L^{\circ}}\to\Omega_{\frac{R_{n-1}/(f)}{K^{\circ}}}\otimes L^{\circ}\to\Omega_{\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\to 0\to 0.

Thus,

\cont\Omega_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{c}-v^{\prime}(f)+v^{\prime}(f^{\prime})+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{v^{\prime},>0}\right).

The exact same argument shows that
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}
is concentrated in degree
0
and that

\cont\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)}{K^{\circ}}}=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{c}-v^{\prime}(f)+v^{\prime}(f^{\prime}).

If
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an ordinary augmentation, then the result follows from noting that
R^{\prime}/(\theta^{-1}f)=L^{\circ}
and that
\const R^{\prime}\to L^{\circ}
logifies to
\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ}
.

If
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is an almost stable limit augmentation on the continuous family
\mathfrak{b}=(\mathfrak{b}_{i})_{i\in I}
, then we may choose multiplicative sets
S_{i}
as in
??
, write

, and write

.
We write

.
Note that
(\const R_{i}\to\tilde{R}_{i})
logifies to

.
As we have just computed

\displaystyle\cont\Omega_{{\tilde{R}_{i}}/{K^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}-v_{i}(f)+v_{i}(f^{\prime})+\left(\inf\Gamma_{K,>0}-\inf\Gamma_{v_{i},>0}\right)

(4.1.d)

\displaystyle\cont\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{{\tilde{R}_{i}}/{K^{\circ}}}

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}-v_{i}(f)+v_{i}(f^{\prime}).

We will show that
\left(\Omega_{{\tilde{R}_{i}}/{K^{\circ}}}\right)_{i\in I}
, and
\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{{\tilde{R}_{i}}/{K^{\circ}}}\right)_{i\in I}
stabilize.

Consider the function
|f|_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}:\left[[\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}](\psi),\infty\right]\to\mathbb{R}(\infty)
, is a piecewise linear function whose slope becomes constant to
s
from
\lambda_{0}
onwards, see
??
.
Clearly

because
f
is unstable for
\mathfrak{b}
.
Suppose that

, by evaluating
|f|_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}
in
(\gamma_{i})
we see that

v_{i}(f)-\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}\geq s\cdot(\gamma_{i}-[\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}](\psi))+[\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}](f)-(\gamma_{i}-[\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}](\psi)),

where the right hand side tends to
\infty
as
i
increases.
Further we know that

\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\frac{R_{i}/(\theta^{-1}_{i}f)}{K^{\circ}}}=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n-2}+\step\mathfrak{b}_{i}-v_{i}(f)+v_{i}(f^{\prime})\geq 0

which is non-negative as it is the content of a module. Thus,
v_{i}(f^{\prime})\to\infty
.
As
\deg f^{\prime}<\deg f
, we know that
(v_{i}(f^{\prime}))
stabilizes to
\infty
and therefore
f^{\prime}=0
, thus
L/K
is inseparable.

Thus,
s=1
whenever
L/K
is separable.
Choose

sufficiently large such that
\Gamma_{v_{i}}
has stabilized had
\gamma_{i}>\lambda_{0}
.
Then let

.
Consider the following commutative diagram with exact rows and columns.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

We will show that the map

\alpha:\frac{L^{\circ}}{(\theta_{i}^{-1}\theta_{j})}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta_{j}^{-1}f)\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle{R_{j}}/{R_{i}}}=\frac{L^{\circ}}{\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j}}\dlog(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j}),

is an isomorphism, where
\kappa
is a constant with
v_{i}(\kappa)=v_{i}(\psi_{j})=\gamma_{i}
, and we have used that
v_{j}=[v_{i},\psi_{j},\gamma_{j}]
and
??
.
Let

be the
\psi_{j}
expansion of

.
As the slope of

has stabilized to
1
we see that
a_{1}\psi_{j}^{1}
is the term with minimal valuation for
v_{j}
.
Then we see that
\theta_{j}^{-1}f=\sum_{s=0}^{m}\theta^{-1}a_{s}\kappa^{s}(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})^{s}
and as an element of
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle{R_{j}}/{R_{i}}}
we compute

\mathrm{d}(\theta_{j}^{-1}f)=\left(\sum_{s=1}^{m}\theta_{j}^{-1}a_{s}\kappa^{s}(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})^{s-1}\right)\cdot\mathrm{d}(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})=\left(\sum_{s=1}^{m}\theta_{j}^{-1}a_{s}\kappa^{s}(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})^{s}\right)\cdot\dlog(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})

We easily check that in the last sum the term
\theta_{j}^{-1}a_{1}\kappa(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j})^{k}
has the lowest valuation equal to
0
, and thus the sum is a unit in
L^{\circ}
.
So
\alpha
takes

to a generator of

\alpha
is surjective.
We find that

w(\theta_{i}^{-1}\theta_{j})=|f|_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}(\gamma_{j})-|f|_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}(\gamma_{i})=\gamma_{j}-\gamma_{i}=w(\kappa^{-1}\psi_{j}).

Here we used that the slope of
|f|_{\mathfrak{a}_{n}}
in this region is
1
.

As
\alpha
is an isomorphism, the snake lemma shows that
H_{1}(\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\tilde{R}_{j}/\tilde{R}_{i}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})=\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\tilde{R}_{j}/\tilde{R}_{i}}\otimes L^{\circ}=\{0\}
.
The same is also true for

as it is isomorphic to
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\tilde{R}_{j}/\tilde{R}_{i}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}
, see
??
.
Then the tower of (log) rings
K^{\circ}\to\tilde{R}_{i}\to\tilde{R}_{j}
shows that
\Omega_{\tilde{R}_{j}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ}=\Omega_{\tilde{R}_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ}
and thus the system
(\Omega_{\tilde{R}_{j}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ})_{j\in I}
stabilizes for

, likewise for the log differentials.
Taking the colimit, we see that

\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}=\Omega_{\tilde{R}_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ},\quad\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}=\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\tilde{R}_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ},

whose content were computed in (
4.1.d
).
∎
