4.2
Purely transcendental extensions and (log) discrepancies

Now we study the case that
L=(K(T),w)
is a purely transcendental field extension of
(K,v_{K})
.

4.2.1 Proposition
.

With notation as above.

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\textstyle\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\simeq\left(\Omega_{\frac{L^{\circ}}{K^{\circ}}}\right)[0],\quad\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\simeq\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\right)[0].

Proof.

Let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain on a simple Gauss semi-valuation
v_{0}
approximating
w
.
Let
S_{i},R_{i}
as in
??
.
We know that
R_{0}=K^{\circ}[T^{\prime}]
for some linear polynomial
T^{\prime}
.
Then

\mathbb{L}_{R_{0}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\simeq\left(L\cdot\mathrm{d}T^{\prime}\right)[0].

We also know that
\mathbb{L}_{L^{\circ}/R_{0}}
and
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})/(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}
are concentrated in degree
0
by
??
and
??
.
Then we conclude by taking the distinguished triangle of (log) cotangent complexes associated to the tower
K^{\circ}\to R_{0}\to L^{\circ}
.
∎

4.2.2

Clearly the module of (log) differentials
\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
is of rank
1
and hence the content is infinite for any such field extension, and this is not a useful notion.
However, the (log) discrepancy still yields a useful invariant of a similar flavor.

4.2.3 Definition
.

Let
(A,v)
be any scalable
K
algebra, and
(L,v_{L})
a valued field with with morphism of semi-valued rings
(A,v)\to(L,v_{L})
.
Let
\omega\in\Omega_{A^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
.
Then we define the
discrepancy
of
\omega
as

\disc a=\cont\left(\frac{\Omega_{A^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ}}{L^{\circ}\cdot w}\right).

Likewise, let
M_{A}=\const A^{\circ}
, or
M_{A}=\bls A^{\circ}
when defined, and let
\omega\in\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{A}\to A^{\circ})/(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}
then we define the
log discrepancy
of
\omega
as

\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\omega=\cont\left(\frac{\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{A}\to A^{\circ})/(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}\otimes L^{\circ}}{L^{\circ}\cdot w}\right).

4.2.4 Remark
.

Note that the choice of
L
in
??
does not matter.
Indeed, consider
F=\mathrm{frac}(A)
, then naturally
F\subset L
and
L^{\circ}
is flat over
F^{\circ}
.
So by
??
, the (log) discrepancy is the same for either choice.

4.2.5 Theorem
.

Let
(L,v_{L})=(K[T]_{\ker w},w)
for some semi-valuation
w
on
K[T]
.
Note that if
\ker w=\{0\}
then
L
is a purely transcendental field extension.
Let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain starting on a Gauss semi-valuation
v_{0}
with center
a
and log-radius
\mu
. We write
\varphi_{0}=T-a
.

(i)

If
\mu\in\Gamma_{K}
, i.e.
v_{0}
is a simple Gauss valuation,

\displaystyle\disc\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}+(\inf\Gamma_{v_{K},>0}-\inf\Gamma_{w,>0})

\displaystyle\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})

\displaystyle=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n},

where
\theta\in K
with
v_{K}(\theta)=\mu
.

(ii)

If
\mu=w(\varphi_{0})
then

\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\dlog(\varphi_{0})=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}.

Proof.

We first show (i).
Let
R_{0}=(K[T],v_{0})^{\circ}
. Then
R_{0}=K^{\circ}[\theta^{-1}(T-a)]
.
We know that

\mathbb{L}_{R_{0}/K^{\circ}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\simeq\left(R_{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})\right).

Recall that we have computed
\mathbb{L}_{L^{\circ}/R_{0}}
in
??
and the logarithmic version in
??
.
By the vanishing of the higher homology in said complexes, the first exact sequence associated to
K^{\circ}\to R_{0}\to L^{\circ}
is short exact and reads

0\to L^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})\to\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\to\Omega_{L^{\circ}/R_{0}}\to 0.

Thus,

\disc\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})=\cont\left(\Omega_{L^{\circ}/R_{0}}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}+(\inf\Gamma_{v_{K}}-\inf(\left<\Gamma_{w})\right>\ .

The same argument computes the log discrepancy.

We move on to (ii). Let
\mu_{-1}\in\Gamma_{K,\leq\mu_{0}}
, and let
v_{-1}
be
[K,\varphi_{0},\mu_{-1}]
.
Write
R_{-1}:=(K[T],v_{-1})^{\circ}=K^{\circ}[\kappa^{-1}\varphi_{0}]
, and
R_{0}=(K[T,\varphi^{-1}],v_{0})^{\circ}
with
\kappa\in K
such that
v_{K}(\kappa)=\mu_{-1}
.
Then
R_{0}=[R_{-1},\varphi,\mu_{0}]
which is scalable.
Let
M_{-1}=\const K^{\circ}\oplus(\kappa^{-1}\varphi_{0})
and consider the log ring
(M_{-1}\to R_{-1})
.
Note that
M_{-1}
is the same log structure as
M_{0}
in the proof of
??
, where we have shown that
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})/(M_{-1}\to R_{-1})}\simeq 0
.
So the tower of log rings
(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})\to(M_{-1}\to R_{-1})\to(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})
shows that

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{-1}\to R_{-1})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}R_{0}\simeq(R_{0}\cdot\dlog(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0}))[0].

Then, the exact sequence of log differentials corresponding to
K^{\circ}\to R_{0}\to L^{\circ}
reads

0\to L^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{d}(\theta^{-1}\varphi_{0})\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}\to\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\to 0,

thus,

\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\dlog(\varphi_{0})=\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const R_{0}\to R_{0})}}\right)=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}.

∎

4.2.6

Note that if
\ker w
is non-trivial, then
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{1}^{n}=\infty
.
So the (log) discrepancy of
\mathrm{d}f
with
f
a linear polynomial that does not generate
\ker w
will always be
\infty
.
However, if we equip
(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}
with the boundary log structure then there are still elements for which the log discrepancy is a meaningful invariant.

4.2.7 Remark
.

If
\ker w=0
then the function
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}
uniquely extends to a semi-valuation on
\Omega_{K(T)/K}
, see
??
.
As
\Omega_{K(T)/K}
is a one dimensional vector space, the value of
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}
of one non-zero element is sufficient to fix it for any element.

4.2.8 Remark
.

We can interpret
??
geometrically on the valuative tree.
Recall that if
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
is an augmentation chain on
v_{0}
approximating
w
, then we may think of
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
as the distance between
v_{0}
and
w
.
So, for any linear polynomial
aT+b
in
L^{\circ}

\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\mathrm{d}(aT+b)
measures the distance between
w
and the Gauss valuation
v_{0}=[v_{K},T+b/a,-v(a)]
.
Likewise,
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\dlog(aT+b)
measures the minimal distance from
w
to the line
\{[v_{K},aT+b,\lambda]\mid\lambda\in\mathbb{R}(\infty)\}
.

This can be made precise when
K
is complete and the valuative tree is identified with the Berkovich affine line, in which case the log discrepancy is a generalization of the weight function as defined in
[
10
]
.
Then
\dlog(aT+b)
is the unique canonical form up to scaling on the marked projective line
(\mathbb{P}_{K}^{1,\text{an}},\{\infty,-b/a\})
.
The line
\{[v_{K},aT+b,\lambda]\mid\lambda\in\mathbb{R}(\infty)\}
gives precisely the skeleton of
(\mathbb{P}_{K}^{1,\text{an}},\{\infty,-b/a\})
, and
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\dlog(aT+b)
measures how far a point is from the skeleton.
The author will explore this in upcoming work.

4.2.9 Theorem
.

Let
w
be a semi-valuation on
K[T]
with
\ker w=(f)
.
We assume
f
to be a monic polynomial and equip
(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}
with the boundary log structure.
Then
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\dlog f=\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(K[T]/(f),w)/K}
.

Proof.

Write
(M_{A}\to A)
for the log ring
(\bls(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ}\to(K[T]_{\ker w},w)^{\circ})
and
L=K[T]/(f)

When no log structure is written in the arguments of the log differentials, we assume that the rings are equipped with the total log structure.
We may again consider a
w
-optimal augmentation chain
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
on a simple Gauss semi-valuation
v_{0}
, approximating
w
with
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
either an ordinary augmentation or an almost stable limit augmentation, and in both cases with (limit) key polynomial
f
.
If
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],f,\infty)
is an ordinary augmentation then
??
shows that

\frac{\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{A}\to A)/K^{\circ}}}{A\cdot\dlog f}\otimes L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{A}\to A)}{(\const K^{\circ}\oplus f^{\mathbb{N}}\to K^{\circ}[f])}}\otimes L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}.

The equality follows from comparing the content of these modules.

Now assume that
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],(\psi,\gamma)_{i\in I},f,\infty)
is an almost stable limit augmentation.
Let
S_{i}
,
R_{i},\tilde{R}_{i},v_{i}
and
\theta_{i}
be as in the almost stable limit case in the proof of
??
.
Let
M_{i}
be the boundary log structure on
R_{i}
.
Then like above we see that

\frac{\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{(M_{i}\to R_{i})/K^{\circ}}}{R_{i}\cdot\dlog f}\otimes L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{\textstyle\frac{(M_{i}\to A)}{(\const K^{\circ}\oplus f^{\mathbb{N}}\to K^{\circ}[f])}}\otimes L^{\circ}\simeq\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes L^{\circ}.

The result follows from taking the colimit over
i
and comparing content.
∎

4.3
Arbitrary field extensions

The following theorem is a generalization of
[
3
, Theorem 6.5.12]
which also includes the analogous result for the log cotangent complex.

4.3.1 Theorem
.

Let
(L,v_{L})/(K,v_{K})
be any extension of valued fields.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be either
\mathbb{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
or
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})/(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}
.
Then
H_{1}(\mathcal{L})
is torsion free and for any
i\geq 2
,
H_{i}(\mathcal{L})=\{0\}
.
Moreover,
H_{1}(\mathcal{L})
is trivial if and only if
L/K
is separable.

Proof.

We write
\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
for either the (non-log) cotangent complex or the log cotangent complex, where
L^{\circ}
and
K^{\circ}
are equipped with their respective total log structure.
We first show that

•

H_{\geq 2}(\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
vanishes, and

•

H_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
is torsion free and vanishes if
L/K
is separable.

These properties will be referred to as
(\star)
.
Later, we will show that
H_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})
does not vanish in the inseparable case.

Suppose that
L/K
is finitely generated, i.e.,
L=K(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
for some
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in L
.
We induct on
n
.
Let
F=K(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1})
.
Consider the tower of (log) rings
K^{\circ}\to F^{\circ}\to L^{\circ}
.
The associated distinguished triangle of (log) cotangent complexes is

\mathcal{L}_{F^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\to\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\to\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/F^{\circ}}.

By induction,
\mathcal{L}_{F^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
satisfies
(\star)
.
The extension
L/F
is either finite or purely transcendental. So
\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
satisfies
(\star)
by
??
and
??
respectively.
From the long exact sequence associated with the distinguished triangle, we easily verify
(\star)
for
\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
.

For general
L/K
we may conclude
(\star)
by taking the colimit over all subfields of
L
that are finitely generated over
K
.

Suppose that
L/K
is inseparable.
Then
H_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})\otimes L\simeq H_{1}(\mathbb{L}_{L/K})\neq 0
.
Therefore,
H_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})\neq 0
.
∎

5
Semi-valuations on differentials and the absolute log different

5.1
The Kähler semi-valuation and key polynomials

5.1.1

Let
(A,v_{A})\to(B,v_{B})
be a morphism of semi-valued rings.
In
[
20
]
, Temkin defines the Kähler norm on
\Omega_{B/A}
as the maximal seminorm such that the map
\mathrm{d}:(B,v_{B})\to(\Omega_{B/A})
is non-expansive.
In our additive language, we define the
Kähler semi-valuation
,
\kv:\Omega_{B/A}\to\mathbb{R}(\infty)
as the minimal semi-valuation on
\Omega_{B/A}
such that
\kv(\mathrm{d}b)\geq v_{B}(b)
for all
b\in B
.
When considering multiple morphisms of rings, we might decorate
\kv
as
\kv_{B/A}
or
\kv_{v_{B}}
to avoid confusion.

5.1.2

One easily checks that the Kähler semi-valuation is compatible with localization in the following way.
Let
S
be a multiplicative set of
B
with
\infty\not\in v_{B}(S)
, then
\kv_{S^{-1}B/A}:\Omega_{S^{-1}B/A}\to\mathbb{R}(\infty):\omega/s\mapsto\kv_{B/A}(\omega)-w(s)
.

5.1.3

Now suppose that
(L,v_{L})/(K,v_{K})
is an extension of valued fields.
Then
\kv_{L/K}
is related to the
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
as follows.
The morphism
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\to\Omega_{L/K}
naturally identifies
(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})_{tf}
as a semilattice in
\Omega_{L/K}
, and this semilattice is an almost unit ball of
\kv_{L/K}
.
This is easily seen from the observation that
\kv(\dlog f)\geq 0
is equivalent to
\kv f\geq v_{L}(f)
for every
f\in L
.
See
[
20
, Theorem 5.1.8]
for more details.

5.1.4

A priori, evaluating the Kähler semi-valuation seems like a difficult problem.
When
B
is uncountable,
\kv
is defined in terms of an uncountable number of inequalities
\kv(\mathrm{d}b)\geq v_{B}(b)
for any
b\in B
.
One may wonder whether there is a smaller and more tractable subset of
B
which is sufficient to define
\kv
.
The main result of this section
??
roughly states that when
(A,v_{A})=(K,v_{K})
and
(B,v_{B})=(K[T],w)
, such a set is given by the key polynomials in a MacLane-Vaquié chain approximating
w
.

5.1.5

For the rest of this section, let
(K[T],w)
be a semi-valued
(K,v_{K})
algebra with
\ker w=\{0\}
.
Let
A\subset K[T]
be a subset.
A semi-value
u
on
\Omega_{K[T]/K}=K[T]\cdot\mathrm{d}T
is
A
-non-expansive
if for any
a\in A
we have that
w(\mathrm{d}a)\geq v(a)
.
We say
u
is
non-expansive
if it is
K[T]
-non-expansive.
Let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain on a simple Gauss valuation
v_{0}=[v_{K},T-a,w(T-a)]
approximating a valuation
w^{\prime}
.
Let
\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n})
be the set consisting of
T-a
and all key polynomials in
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, including the key polynomials in the unstable families of the limit augmentations.
Further, we write
K[T]_{v_{n}=w}=\{f\in K[T]\mid v_{n}(f)=w\}
.

5.1.6 Lemma
.

With notation as above, let
u
be a semi-valuation on
\Omega_{K[T]/K}
.
Then, the following statements are equivalent,

(i)

u
is
\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n})
non-expansive,

(ii)

u
is
K[T]_{w^{\prime}=w}
non-expansive.

Proof.

As usual, we write
v_{i}=[\mathfrak{a}_{i}]
.
Note that
\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n})
is a subset of
K[T]_{v_{n}=w}
and thus the implication from (ii)
\implies
(i) is trivial.
We will prove the implication (i)
\implies
(ii) by induction on
n
.
First suppose that
n=0
, and let
f\in(K[T],v_{0})
be
w
-stable.
Let
f=\sum_{i=0}^{m}a_{i}(T-a)^{i}
be the
T-a
expansion.
Then
f^{\prime}\cdot(T-a)=\sum_{i=0}^{m}i\cdot a_{i}(T-a)^{i}
.
Clearly

w(f)=v_{0}(f)\leq v_{0}(f^{\prime}(T-a))\leq w(f^{\prime}(T-a))\leq w(f^{\prime})\cdot u(\mathrm{d}T-a)=u(\mathrm{d}f),

thus
u
is
K[T]_{v_{0}=w}
-non-expansive.

Suppose that the theorem holds for chains of length
n-1
.
We first consider the case in which
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=(v_{n-1},\varphi_{n},\mu_{n})
is an ordinary augmentation.
Let
f\in K[T]_{v_{n}=w}
and let
f=\sum_{i=0}^{m}a_{i}\varphi_{n}^{i}
be the
\varphi_{n}
expansion.
Then

\displaystyle w(f)=v_{0}(f)

\displaystyle=\min_{i}\{i\cdot w(\varphi_{n})+w(a_{i})\}

\displaystyle\leq\min_{i}\{iw(\varphi_{n})+u(\mathrm{d}a_{i}),w(a_{i})+(i-1)w(\varphi_{n})+u(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{n})\}

\displaystyle\leq u\left(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\varphi_{n}^{i}\mathrm{d}a_{i}+a_{i}\cdot i\cdot\varphi_{n}^{i-1}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{n}\right)=u(\mathrm{d}f).

Here we use that
u(\mathrm{d}\varphi_{n})\geq w(\varphi_{n})
as
u
is
\varphi_{n}
-non-expansive, and that
u(\mathrm{d}a_{i})\geq w(a_{i})
due to the induction hypothesis and the fact that
\deg a_{i}<\deg\varphi_{n}
, thus
a_{i}\in K[T]_{v_{n-1}=w}
.
If
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=(v_{n-1},(\psi_{j},\gamma_{j})_{j\in J})
is a stable continuous family, then the same holds when using the
\psi_{j}
expansion of
f
where
i
is chosen such that
[v_{n-1},\psi_{j},\gamma_{j}](f)=w(f)
.

Now suppose that
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=(v_{n-1},(\psi_{j},\gamma_{j})_{j\in J},\varphi_{n},\mu_{n})
is a limit augmentation.
Again, let
f\in K[T]_{v_{n}=w}
and let
f=\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}\varphi_{n}^{i}
be the
\varphi_{n}
expansion.
Choose some
j\in J
sufficiently large such that
[v_{n-1},\psi_{j},\gamma_{j}](a_{i})=w(a_{i})
for all
a_{i}
.
Write
\mathfrak{b}=(v_{n-1},\psi_{j},\gamma_{j})
.
Then
(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{b})
is a
w
-optimal augmentation chain of length
n
for which we have just shown that the theorem holds.
As
a_{i}\in K[T]_{[\mathfrak{b}]=w}
we know that
u(\mathrm{d}a_{i})\geq w(a_{i})
.
The same computation as in the ordinary case shows that
u(\mathrm{d}f)\geq w(f)
, hence the theorem holds for finite
n
.

Finally, we assume that
n=\infty
.
Then the values
(v_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
have a stable limit.
Let
f\in K[T]_{w^{\prime}=w}
, then for some sufficiently large
m
,
f\in K[T]_{v_{m}=w}
.
Then
u
is
\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{m})
-non-expansive, and thus
u(\mathrm{d}f)\geq w(f)
.
∎

5.1.7 Theorem
.

Let
w
be a valuation on
K[T]
and let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain approximating
w
on a Gauss valuation
v_{0}=[v_{K},T-a,w(T-a)]
.
Then
\kv_{w}
is the unique semi-valuation on
\Omega_{K[T]/K}
as a
(K[T],w)
-module with

\kv_{w}(\mathrm{d}T)=\sup\{w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys\left((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\right)\}.

Proof.

By
??

\kv
is the minimal semi-valuation for which
\kv(\mathrm{d}\varphi)=w(\varphi^{\prime})+\kv(\mathrm{d}T)\geq w(\varphi)
for every
\varphi\in\keys\left((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\right)
.
Equivalently,
\kv
is the minimal semi-valuation for which
\kv(\mathrm{d}T)\geq w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})
for each
\varphi\in\keys\left((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\right)
, from which the theorem follows.
∎

5.1.8 Remark
.

We give this proof of
??
, because it is very elementary.
However, we can also deduce this from the computations in
??
.
We sketch an alternative proof.
The set
\{\dlog\varphi\mid\varphi\in\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n})\}
generates
\Omega^{\text{log}}_{K(T)^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
by
??
.
For this module to be an almost unit ball of
\kv
,
\kv
has to be the smallest semi-valuation such that
\kv(\dlog\varphi)\geq 0
for all
\varphi\in\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n})
.
The condition
\kv(\dlog\varphi)\geq 0
is equivalent to
\kv(\mathrm{d}\varphi)\geq w(\varphi)
which is equivalent to
\kv(\mathrm{d}T)\geq w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})
.

5.2
The absolute log different

5.2.1 Definition
.

Let
L/K
be an extension of valued fields.
The
absolute
log different of
L/K
is

\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{L/K}=\cont\left(\left(\Omega_{\frac{(\const L^{\circ}\to L^{\circ})}{(\const K^{\circ}\to K^{\circ})}}^{\text{log}}\right)_{\text{tor}}\right),

when
L/K
is separable and
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{L/K}=\infty
when
L/K
is inseparable.

5.2.2

This is a version of the log different that also makes sense for non-algebraic field extensions and was introduced in
[
20
]
.
Note that in
[
20
]
this is simply called the log different.
But as this is quite a more subtle invariant than the classical log different, we prefer to give it a different name.

5.2.3 Proposition
.

Let
(L,v_{L})/(K,v_{K})
be a separable extension of transcendence degree 1 (e.g.,
L=K(T)
) and let
\omega\in\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
.
Then
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\omega=\kv(\omega_{L})+\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}
, where
\omega_{L}
denotes the image of
\omega
in
\Omega_{L/K}
.

Proof.

If
\omega
is torsion, then
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\omega=\infty
and
\omega_{L}=0
, thus
\kv\omega_{L}=\infty
and the equality holds.
Suppose that
\omega
is not torsion.
Then we easily verify that the following sequence is SES.

0\to\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\right)_{\text{tor}}\to\frac{\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}}{L^{\circ}\cdot\omega}\to\frac{\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\right)_{\text{tf}}}{L^{\circ}\cdot\omega}\to 0.

The content of the first two modules are by definition
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}
and
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\omega
respectively.
As
\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}\right)_{\text{tf}}
is an almost unit ball of
\kv
we see that the content of the third module is
\kv\omega_{L}
.
The result follows from the additivity of content.
∎

5.2.4 Remark
.

When
K
is complete and discretely valued and
L
discretely valued then
\kv(\omega_{L})+\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{L/K}
is actually equal to the weight of
\omega
in
[
10
]
, see
[
20
, Theorem 8.3.3]
.
So one may characterize the weight semi-valuation
\wt
on
\Omega_{L/K}
as the unique semi-valuation such that
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}(\omega)=\wt(\omega_{L})
for every
\omega\in\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
.

5.2.5

If we can find an
\omega\in\Omega_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}}
such that
\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}\omega
,
\kv\omega_{L}
are computable and
\kv\omega_{L}\neq\infty
, then we can also compute the absolute log different
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{L/K}
.
This is essentially what the first part of the following theorem states.
The next part shows that even if there are no such elements, we can still compute the absolute log different by considering the log different on intermediate approximations of
w
, which on Berkovich spaces can be seen as a sort of continuity property of the absolute log different at type IV and non-rigid type I points.

5.2.6 Theorem
.

Let
w
be a valuation on
L=K(T)
and let
(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be a
w
-optimal augmentation chain on a Gauss valuation
v_{0}=[v_{K},\varphi_{0},w(\varphi_{0})]
approximating
w
.
If
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}<\infty
, then

\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(K(T),w)/K}=\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}+w(\varphi_{0})-\sup\{w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys\left((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\right)\}.

If
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}=\infty
, then

\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),w)/K}=\lim_{j\in I}\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),v_{j})/K},

where either

•

n=\infty
,
I=\mathbb{N}
, and
v_{j}=[\mathfrak{a}_{j}]
or

•

n
is finite,
\mathfrak{a}_{n}=([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],(\psi_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i\in I})
is a stable limit, and
v_{j}=[[\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],\psi_{j},\gamma_{j}]
.

Moreover,
(\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),v_{j})/K})_{j\in I}
is non-decreasing, and each element of the sequence can be computed as in the first case.

5.2.7

Note that the case in which
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}=\infty
and
n<\infty
does not occur when
K
is complete.
When
n=\infty
, then the result can be written in the perhaps simpler form,

\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),w)/K}=\lim_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{j}+w(\varphi_{0})-\sup\{w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys((\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{j})\}.

Proof of
??
.

First we assume that
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}<\infty
.
Then by
??
,
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{log}}_{(K(T),w)/K}=\operatorname{disc}^{\mathrm{log}}(\dlog\varphi_{0})-\kv(\dlog\varphi_{0})
as long as one of the terms on the right is finite.
The result now follows by filling in the results of
??
and part (ii) of
??
.

We now treat the case where
\step(\mathfrak{a}_{i})=\infty
.
We assume that all log rings are equipped with their standard log structure.
Recall that there is an increasing union of
K^{\circ}
algebras
R_{i},i\in I
such that
(K(T),w)^{\circ}
is the localization obtained by inverting all elements of valuation
0
in
R_{\infty}=\bigcup_{i}R_{i}
, and for each
j\geq i\in I
,
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{j}/R_{i}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}=\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{j}/R_{i}}\otimes L^{\circ}[0]
.
When
n=\infty
, this is due to Theorems
3.3.6
and
3.5.7
and when
n<\infty
and
\mathfrak{a}_{n}
is a stable continuous family, this is due to Lemmas
3.3.5
and
3.5.5
.
Write
R_{0}=(K[T,\varphi_{0}^{-1}],v_{0})^{\circ}
.
Moreover, when
n=\infty
we let
\mathfrak{A}_{j}=(\mathfrak{a}_{i})_{i=1}^{j}
and when
n<\infty
we let
\mathfrak{A}_{j}=\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{a}_{n-1},([\mathfrak{a}_{n-1}],\psi_{j},\gamma_{j})\right)
.
Then
\mathfrak{A}_{j}
approximates
v_{j}
.

For
j\geq i
, we may consider the map of SES,

Note that the middle modules are simply
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/R_{0}}\otimes L^{\circ}
and
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{j}/R_{0}}\otimes L^{\circ}
, which have content
\step\mathfrak{A}_{i}
,
\step\mathfrak{A}_{j}
respectively.
We then also see that
g
is injective by the vanishing of
H_{1}(\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{j}/R_{i}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})
, and thus
f
is also injective and the family
((\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})_{\text{tor}})_{i\in I}
is an increasing union with colimit
(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{L^{\circ}/K^{\circ}})_{\text{tor}}
.

By
??
, we already see that
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{L/K}=\lim_{i}\cont\left((\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})_{\text{tor}}\right)
and that the limit is non-decreasing.
It remains to be shown that
\cont\left((\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})_{\text{tor}}\right)=\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),v_{i})/K}
.

Moreover, by
??
, we know that
\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}
is generated by
\{\dlog\varphi,\varphi\in\keys\mathfrak{A}_{i}\}
, and therefore
(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ})_{\text{tf}}
maps to an almost unit ball of the minimal semi-valuation on
\Omega_{L/K}
that is
\keys(\mathfrak{A}_{i})
-non-expansive, denoted by
u_{i}
, which like in
??
is determined by

u_{i}(\mathrm{d}T)=\sup\{w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys\mathfrak{A}_{i}\}.

Then, the additivity of content states

\displaystyle\cont\left(\Omega^{\mathrm{log}}_{R_{i}/K^{\circ}}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}L^{\circ}\right)_{\text{tor}}

\displaystyle=\step\mathfrak{A}_{i}+w(\varphi_{0})-\sup\{w(\varphi)-w(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys\mathfrak{A}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=\step\mathfrak{A}_{i}+v_{i}(\varphi_{0})-\sup\{v_{i}(\varphi)-v_{i}(\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi\in\keys\mathfrak{A}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}^{\mathrm{abs,log}}_{(K(T),v_{i})/K}.

∎
